- **Natural disengagement:** There is no Abort Dive state, timeout, cooldown, forced evacuation vector, special sprint, attack suppression, or Abort Dive UI/event. When an established fight becomes unfavorable, normal decision commitment and the terrain-aware retreat planner choose whether and where to disengage at ordinary movement speed. A bad commitment can still be lethal.
- Focused coverage in `src/towerDiveLimits.test.mjs` covers a favorable coordinated commitment, an unfavorable decline without an abort state, and safe bush/perimeter behavior. Verification remains deferred to the requested final combined pass.

## Nature Link (Tequoia Skill 2) Invariants & Status Representation

- **Enemy-Only Targeting:**
  - Tequoia's Skill 2 (`Nature Link` / `Forest Link`) exclusively targets and links enemy champions (`e.team !== u.team`). Allied champions are never linked and never take shared damage.
  - AI cast logic in `combatDecision.ts` evaluates enemy clumping ($\ge 2$ hostile champions within 125px or $\ge 1$ in an active teamfight) without requiring friendly count checks, allowing aggressive usage even with melee allies engaging in the center.
- **Shared Damage Clutter Elimination:**
  - When linked enemy champions take shared health damage (10% true damage propagated to all other living linked enemies), repetitive floating text labels (`Nature Link -XX` / `Forest Link -XX`) are suppressed in `applyDamageToChampion`.
  - This eliminates visual screen clutter when 4 or more heroes are linked, keeping the battlefield clean and legible while preserving full health bar depletion and combat logs.
- **Unique Status Crest & Visual Links:**
  - The overhead rectangular `'LINKED'` text badge is replaced with a custom circular emerald crest icon at `(u.x, barY - 18)`.
  - The icon features interlocking vine chain rings, an emerald leaf bud accent, and a radial arc that ticks down with remaining link duration.
- Regression tests in `src/combatDecision.test.mjs` verify AI cast decisions with clustered allies and enemies.

## Equalized 100 OVR Draft Mode (Normal Game Â· No Rank)

- **Squad Lineup Bypassed & 100 OVR Normalization:**
  - In Clash Arena, players can launch `Equalized Draft (Normal Â· 100 OVR Â· No Rank)`. In this mode, user squad cards, bench cards, and collection ratings are completely bypassed.
  - Every player card in the game (the pool of 63 athletes) is equalized to 100 Overall GOAT tier with 100 in all stats (`lan`, `tf`, `iq`, `clu`, `sta`, `flx`), all combat roles unlocked (`playableRoles`), and all avatars registered as signatures.
  - `playerCardCombatPower` yields an identical maximum multiplier of 1.05 for every avatar, creating complete mechanical stat parity.
  - Both teams are guided by equalized Master Tactician coaches (`EQUALIZED_COACH_BLUE` and `EQUALIZED_COACH_RED`) with identical 10 playbook and 10 chemistry bonuses.
- **Two-Phase Draft Flow:**
  - **Phase 1: Player Card Draft (`PlayerDraftPhaseView`):**
    - Blue (User) and Red (Opponent AI) participate in a 10-turn Snake Draft (`PLAYER_DRAFT_TURNS`: Blue -> Red -> Red -> Blue -> Blue -> Red -> Red -> Blue -> Blue -> Red).
    - Coaches draft 5 starting athletes across TOP, JUNGLE, MID, BOT, and SUPPORT slots.
    - AI Coach evaluates complementary roles, trait synergies (`Shotcaller`, `Clutch King`, `Aggro Diver`, `One-Tap God`), and origin chemistry.
    - Supports card search, role/origin filters, slot targeting, and an "Auto-Draft Rest" button for quick simulation.
  - **Phase 2: Avatar Draft (`DraftPhaseView`):**
    - The drafted 100 OVR Blue and Red rosters sit on stage to draft their avatars via standard ban/pick phases.
    - Coaches can return to Phase 1 (`â† Back to Player Draft`) at any time before avatars lock in.
- **Pure Draft Outcome & Zero Rating Risk:**
  - Because all 10 players have identical 100 stats, victory is determined purely by drafting strategy, player trait moves, avatar synergies, and arena execution.
  - The mode is strictly a Normal Game: Chess Elo rating is never risked or modified.
  - Normal multiplayer rooms (Vs Player) also feature an `Equalize to 100 OVR` toggle to enable casual equalized friendlies between players.
- Regression tests in `src/equalizedMode.test.mjs` verify 100 OVR generation, Cardrel parody alias preservation, stat parity (1.05 power across avatars), snake draft turn structure, AI pick complementary role selection, and coach equity.

## Ability and Teamfight Decisions (Current)

- Nullweaver's Singularity Well has a 105px pull radius and 175px cast reach. Corsara's Broadside Waltz is a repeated-wave channel that crowd control or silence can interrupt. Faelith, Oathmute, Cloudtail and Stonebranch have separate original kits; Cloudtail and Stonebranch are brothers in the game's lore. `docs/ability-balance-review.md` lists the per-avatar balance observations and proposed measurements.
- A team remembers an enemy Q, W or R cooldown only after a nearby living ally sees that ability used. Oathmute's global ultimate reveals its own cooldown to both teams, without revealing unseen Q/W casts. Knowledge expires when the observed cooldown is expected to finish. Teams presume unknown enemy abilities may be ready.
- Higher-IQ cards use remembered ultimate and key-skill downtime to choose fight targets, judge incoming damage, time Black Hole spacing and bait an enemy skill from cover. Objective contests use the same target knowledge. Cards with lower IQ make these reads less consistently. Ratings change decisions, not combat stats.
- An allied channel is an engage opportunity only for teammates who can survive their local fight. The retreat check compares nearby pressure, available personal damage, allied help and reachable targets. A low-health enemy is not a safe finish when multiple nearby opponents can punish it. Clutch and Unkillable traits obey the losing-fight check.
- `src/cooldownKnowledge.test.mjs` and `src/blackHoleCounterplay.test.mjs` cover visibility, global casts, memory expiry, IQ-dependent retreat and target choice, multi-enemy finish bait, and Black Hole danger. `npm run check:game` passed 153 tests plus design validation and TypeScript; `npm run build` passed.
- Cooldown estimates do not follow hidden cooldown resets or post-cast haste changes. Balance and teamfight timing still need a complete stable live match batch.

## Respawn and Hook Travel

- Champion death timers use `calculateDeathTimer` in `src/combatPacingRules.ts` for both combat and fountain deaths. The current checkpoints are 8 seconds at level 3/1:00, 13 seconds at level 10/4:00, 20 seconds at level 15/8:00, 27 seconds at level 18/12:00, and a hard cap of 28 seconds.
- Mirehook, Voltgrip, and Wraithhook Skill 1 remain dodgeable projectile hooks. Their projectile head and chain are drawn during flight. A hit applies ordinary damage/control and starts a visible grab: the victim travels toward the living caster at 560 world units per second, stopping 43 units away. The pull lasts at most 1.1 seconds, stops if the caster dies, and interrupts recall. No instant position jump is permitted for these hooks.
- `src/combatPacingRules.test.mjs` checks the timer curve and that a hook pull takes several simulation ticks. `npm run check:game` passed 155 tests plus design validation and TypeScript; `npm run build` passed. A live desktop match showed a hook chain in a crowded fight ([frame](hook-live-review.png)); a frame-by-frame pull capture and measured kill-to-structure conversion remain open.

## Raised Base and Gravemarch Siege Rules

- The arena is 2600 world units wide (the user explicitly chose a wider battlefield). Shared `arenaLayout.ts` coordinates place wells at x=65/2535, Nexuses at 235/2365, barracks at 430/2170, third turrets at 610/1990, inner turrets at 830/1770, and outer turrets at 1050/1550. Barracks-to-Nexus spacing is 195, tier-3-to-barracks spacing is 180, and tower-layer spacing is 220. Both teams are mirrored. The raised base extends from the third turret through its well; elevation/mist confer no height bonus. The viewport still fits the whole arena.
- Four entrance bushes at x=540/1460 on the north and south flanks precede the ramps. They follow ordinary bush concealment, ward, and reveal rules. The rocky clusters between camps and epic pits were made solid in the follow-up below.
- The neutral Gravemarch Colossus starts absent, awakens once at 2:00, and never respawns after defeat. A claim queues exactly one friendly siege Golem for the claiming team's next 22-second creep wave. The lane model uses the same 1.7 scale as the neutral model, with its health bar and name raised to fit. Its charge still targets a living enemy tower. Embermaw starts absent and first awakens at 4:00.
- After a champion kill, an AI card may keep a short structure-push call when at least two allies survive, they outnumber living enemies, the next enemy structure is within 900 units, and the actor is healthy and not losing its local fight. High-IQ cards or strong macro coaches keep the read up to 18 seconds at 35% health; others require 48% health and a kill within 12 seconds. While the call applies, shopping recall, optional jungle farming, fresh epic starts, and passive regrouping yield to the lane siege. Dangerous fights still trigger retreat. This is a decision rule, not a damage buff.
- `src/arenaRules.test.mjs`, `src/macroFarmRules.test.mjs`, and `src/siegeGolemRules.test.mjs` cover the new geometry, entrances, push call, and awakening. `npm run check:game` passed all 157 tests, design validation, and TypeScript; `npm run build` passed. A live visual review of the claimed reinforcement remains open.

## Solid Rock and Nexus Volley Rules

- `CAMP_ROCK_RINGS` in `src/arenaRules.ts` defines one nearly circular, raised stone enclosure around each of the eight ordinary camps and both epic pits. Each ring has a lane-facing opening wide enough for an avatar to enter; the two top buff camps were moved outward so their walls do not overlap adjacent camps. Mirrored rows of solid stones frame the north and south sides of each high-ground ramp, leaving its central stair open at lane y=380. Farming AI and Dragon/Golem objective approaches use `rockApproachWaypoint` to reach the open ramp stair and camp gate, including routes from either base to both epic pits. Outside the entrance cone, the waypoint advances avatars around the pit perimeter instead of sending them straight through its rock wall. `ROCK_TERRAIN` supplies both collision and canvas footprints. Champions, minions (including the claimed Colossus and Spirit Bear), and neutral camps cannot occupy these footprints. Movement is swept in short steps to prevent short dashes crossing stone. A teleport longer than 350 units can cross stone but its destination is pushed clear if it lands inside it. Rocks currently block units, not spell projectiles.
- Alive barracks have unique canvas models: a Melee forge with crossed blades, a Ranged watchtower with a drawn bow, and a Catapult siege yard with wheels and a loaded arm. Destroying each barracks retains its matching creep upgrade rule.
- The Nexus is drawn at 1.85 scale with six orbiting emitters. Its existing volley remains five pulses at 0.2-second intervals followed by a 2.2-second reload. Each pulse picks up to six **distinct** targets in range, prioritizing a dive aggressor and then champions before minions, and fires one true-damage shot at each. A unit is never hit six times by one pulse solely because fewer targets are present. The current 85 damage per shot is unchanged.
- `src/arenaRules.test.mjs` checks five-pulse timing, six distinct targets, collision, each camp opening, nearby lane approaches, both central high-ground stairs, and routes from both bases to camps and epic pits. `src/objectiveRules.test.mjs` covers objective starts in early midgame while preserving the wave, vision, and healthy-team gates. `src/siegeGolemRules.test.mjs` checks the 2:00 one-time awakening. The Dragon's first spawn time is 4:00. Rocks do not absorb projectiles; non-objective pursuit still uses local collision rather than a general pathfinder.

## Natural Geological Rock Formations and Cove Embankments

- **Continuous Bedrock Foundations (`drawRaisedRockTerrain` in `AramMatchView.tsx`):**
  - Camp walls are drawn over a continuous curved bedrock embankment along the non-entrance perimeter (`entrance + 0.62` to `entrance + 2*Math.PI - 0.62`).
  - Highground walls have continuous horizontal bedrock shelves connecting the 3 stones in each row.
  - This eliminates isolated "teeth" and embeds all camps naturally into organic cliff coves.
- **Procedural 3D Organic Boulders:**
  - Deterministic coordinate hashing (`rockHash`) varies rock width, height, chiseled vertices, and tilt per stone without any frame jitter.
  - Boulders around camp perimeters align tangentially with the cove curve, naturally wrapping around the clearing.
  - Multi-faceted 3D lighting: soft ground contact shadow, dark under-facet base, midtone body with directional light gradient, sunlit upper-left crest facet, chiseled ridge highlights, and hairline fracture fissures.
- **Biome Theming:**
  - **Dragon Pit (`dragon_boss`):** Scorched basalt/obsidian, glowing molten magma veins (`rgba(249, 115, 22, 0.85)` / `#fed7aa`), amber ember rim lighting, no green vegetation.
  - **Siege Golem Pit (`j_siege_golem`):** Ancient monolithic runic slate with deep teal lichen (`#0d9488`).
  - **Jungle Camps:** Weathered river granite with lush emerald moss patches (`#047857`, `#10b981`) and base scree pebbles.
  - **Highground Base Ramps:** Fortified masonry bedrock ledges.
- **Invariant Protection:** All 132 `ROCK_TERRAIN` positions, radii, and collision tests in `arenaRules.ts` remain identical, ensuring zero regression to pathing, movement, or dash clipping.

## Situational Teamfight, Recovery, and Wave Tempo

- `src/teamTempoRules.ts` judges an allied channel by enemies actually caught, remaining control time versus travel time, uncontrolled threats, allied help, current HP, cast reach, card IQ/teamfight ratings, and tower exposure. A five-target Black Hole lets a wounded ranged avatar follow while dangerous free enemies, tower fire, or a catch that will end before arrival prevent a reckless engage. A decisive catch can interrupt retreat or an active recall, and the caught enemy receives target priority. Black Hole follow-up uses its actual 105px pull radius.
- An available health relic within a short, safe route becomes the immediate task when its heal is meaningful to a wounded avatar. A strong follow-up or healthy wave push keeps priority, and enemies guarding the relic make it unsafe. Critical HP can justify a slightly longer detour when no nearby enemy can stop it.
- A healthy team with a nearby allied wave, favorable local numbers, and a structure in reach can call a wave-led push without a recent kill. Player IQ, lane skill, and coach macro affect the read. This call postpones shopping recall, optional jungle farming, and fresh epic starts. Enemy wave clear protects the crash before structure hits when needed.
- Normal lane advance follows the allied wave front and holds outside an enemy structure's attack range when no wave is present. A healthy target beyond that limit does not bait an unsupported chase. Active objective fights, decisive channel follow-up, and an exposed Nexus retain their own priority rules.
- Team tempo adds a coordinated reset before known objective spawns: 45â€“60 seconds before the next Dragon spawn or Gravemarch's one-time awakening, at least three living teammates must independently need HP/mana or a shop purchase and be able to recall under the existing enemy, minion, structure-distance, and cooldown safety checks. Nearby enemies, recent champion damage, tower aggro, or an active local push block starting that reset. Once called, the plan persists through the reset window so one channeling teammate does not prevent others from joining. Unsafe teammates keep their normal lane, fight, or retreat behavior rather than waiting in mid. In the final 25 seconds, healthy teammates may rally beside the upcoming pit only when their lane wave is already pushed, they have lane priority, and no active fight or nearby enemy prevents setup. Normal warding and objective decision rules remain authoritative.
- Raised rocks render after the battlefield floor and before minions/champions, keeping avatar names, health bars, and bottom avatar labels readable. The design validator checks this draw order. `src/teamTempoRules.test.mjs` covers low-health multi-target follow-up, safe relic diversion, wave tempo, mirrored advance limits, coordinated objective recalls, and healthy pit staging. `npm run check:game` passed design validation, TypeScript, and 169 tests; `npm run build` passed.

## Enlarged Jungle Camps, Flared Gateways, Smooth Tangent Sliding, and Role-Based Farming

- **Spacious Jungle Camp Clearing Geometry:** Standard camp radius is enlarged from $60\text{px}$ to $76\text{px}$, doubling inner camp ground area from $\sim 5,280\text{px}^2$ to $\sim 10,560\text{px}^2$. Boss pits are enlarged to $115\text{px}$ (Gravemarch Golem) and $122\text{px}$ (Dragon Embermaw).
- **Flared Non-Pinch Openings:** Camp gateway boulders are flared outward (`radius + 3px`) with a tapered stone radius ($0.88\times$), ensuring a clear entrance passage of at least $104\text{px}$ wide without inward-curving hook stones. Units within $0.95$ radians ($109^\circ$ cone) or inside the clearing boundary (`distanceToCenter < ring.radius - 8`) navigate directly to their target.
- **Frictionless Tangent Sliding:** Rock collision in `resolveRockTerrainMovement` detects overlaps and continuously slides units along the rock circumference tangent oriented toward their intended goal ($\vec{t}_{\text{goal}} \cdot \vec{d}_{\text{to}} \ge 0$), completely eliminating sticking, shaking, or pinning in crevices.
- **Support Macro Tethering & Team Camp Participation:** Supports remain tethered to their team during safe lane downtime, but may join a coordinated post-recall team camp objective. They still cannot select camps for solo farming.

## Early Epic Starts and Clear Pit Routes

- Epic objective startup keeps its earliest availability, lane-priority, vision, health, ally-count, and nearby-enemy gates, but lowers the macro rating threshold to 54 (50 for a safe low-health finish opportunity). This is intended to let an average coordinated team take Gravemarch when it awakens at 2:00 and Embermaw when it first appears at 4:00, rather than holding starts for very high ratings.
- Dragon Embermaw and Gravemarch approach movement now uses `rockApproachWaypoint`, matching ordinary camp farming. The waypoint guides units through each objective ring's opening and routes around its perimeter when a direct approach would hit stone. The opening and both-base route tests cover both boss pits.
- `npm run check:game` passed design validation, all 172 tests, and TypeScript. `npm run build` passed with the existing large-bundle advisory.
- Limit: non-objective champion pursuit still relies on swept local collision and does not yet have a map-wide pathfinder; rocks still do not block projectiles.

## Camp Team Objectives, Shared Rewards, and Map Polish

- After a safe recall, two or more recently recalled teammates can converge on an available ordinary camp when at least three allies live, two are healthy, the team has no active threat, and no wave-led push is underway. One persistent plan selects a single nearby live camp on the team's own half, records the recalled participants, and remains valid for up to 30 game-seconds after they leave the well. The plan cancels immediately for enemy threats, a lane push, an epic-objective call, fewer than two surviving participants, expiry, or an unavailable camp. The planned group can include Supports. Existing lane pressure, danger, and exposed-Nexus priorities remain higher priority.
- Every champion who damaged a camp for the securing team shares its fixed gold and XP bounty. Each gets an equal integer share; rounding remainder goes to the killer. A soft-reset camp clears its contribution list, and respawn starts a new contribution cycle.
- Camp entrances remain visually open without painted roads or dashed route lines. Rock collision projection iterates across neighboring boulders so clearing one collision does not leave a unit embedded in another.
- `src/macroFarmRules.test.mjs` covers team-call safety, Support participation only on a team call, and non-multiplying reward splits. `src/arenaRules.test.mjs` checks safe projection out of adjacent rocks and both-base camp/objective routes.
- `npm run check:game` passed design validation, all 178 game tests and 3 multi-seed simulation tests, and TypeScript. `npm run build` passed with the existing large-bundle advisory.
- Limit: the headless match simulator does not yet model the live post-recall team-camp call or shared XP distribution. General champion pursuit still relies on local swept collision outside the explicit camp and objective approach routes.

### Coordinated camp-plan polish verification

- `choosePostRecallTeamCamp` gives every recalled participant one deterministic nearby home-side camp instead of allowing independent per-avatar choices.
- `src/macroFarmRules.test.mjs` verifies that both teams choose one shared destination and that a single recalled avatar cannot create a team plan.
- `npm run check:game` passed design validation, all 180 game tests, 3 multi-seed simulation tests, and TypeScript. `npm run build` passed with the existing large-bundle advisory; `git diff --check` passed.

## Headless Multi-Seed Match Simulation and Invariant Telemetry Engine

- `src/matchSimulationEngine.ts` implements a fast, deterministic, headless game simulator allowing batch execution of complete bridge arena matches across seeds.
- **Sequential Structure Vulnerability (`canDamageStructure`):** Enforces strict structure destruction gating (Outer Turret â†’ Inner Turret â†’ Nexus Turret â†’ Barracks â†’ Nexus). Units cannot bypass active forward defenses.
- **Tower Plating & Siege Pacing:** Integrates `towerSiegeMultiplier(sim.matchTime)` within `damageStructure`, providing early tower damage reduction (30% increasing to 100% over 8 minutes) that prevents premature match collapse and stabilizes average match duration at 7.7 minutes (median 5.8m).
- **Risk-Based Tower Combat:** Applies `assessTowerDiveRisk` to a live local tower situation. Headless telemetry reports committed dive opportunities and risk declines; it does not create an abort state or use forced evacuation movement.
- **Turret Execution 10-Second Attribution:** Enforces `resolveTurretKillReward` with the 10-second attribution window. Champion damage within $\le 10$ seconds awards kill and gold to the enemy attacker; damage older than 10 seconds triggers a turret execution splitting 300g (60g each in 5v5) across defending allies with 0 champion kills awarded.
- **Headless simulation scope:** The multi-seed simulator still models only solo camp farming and therefore reports zero Support camp participation; it does not yet model the live post-recall team-camp objective.
- **Cardrel Parody Alias Invariant:** Strictly preserves `Cardrel` parody alias across all simulation lineups and tests.
- **Automated Telemetry Diagnostics (`simulateBatchMatches`):** Aggregates win rates, duration distributions, role damage and kill contributions, mana lockout frequencies, skillshot accuracy, objective contestations, and flags side imbalances or pacing warnings automatically.
- Tested via `npm run test:simulation` (`src/matchSimulation.test.mjs`) and integrated into `npm run check:game`.

## Ability Visual Presentation

- Every avatar keeps its dedicated procedural motif for Skill 1, Skill 2, and Ultimate. A shared presentation layer now adds a soft perspective ground bloom, animated perimeter marking, curved source-to-impact energy trace, source cast ring, deterministic diamond sparks, and an expanding white impact ripple around that motif.
- Ultimates receive the strongest visual hierarchy: more particles, wider trails, brighter bloom, and a rotating twelve-segment seal. Skill 2 remains slightly richer than Skill 1 without competing with ultimate readability.
- Beam and lightning primitives use a colored glow body with a narrow white-hot core. The particle layout is derived from a stable avatar-and-slot seed and never consumes gameplay randomness, so replays remain deterministic and the effect does not flicker between frames.
- These additions are render-only. Ability range, radius, targeting, damage, crowd control, cooldowns, timing, and collision remain unchanged.
- `src/avatarSkillAnimation.test.mjs` renders every one of the 45 avatar motifs at three animation stages for all three slots and verifies stable, increasing VFX profiles. `npm run check:game` passed design validation, all 181 game tests, all 3 multi-seed simulation tests, and TypeScript. `npm run build` passed with the existing large-bundle advisory; `git diff --check` passed.
- Limit: automated canvas tests validate safe rendering and visual hierarchy data, but a live browser capture is still desirable for final bloom and particle-density tuning on different display sizes.

## Developer Random 5v5 Mode

- Development builds expose a `Developer Random 5v5` card beneath the normal Clash Arena modes. One click skips the player-card and avatar drafts and immediately starts a seeded match with ten unique randomly selected player cards and ten unique randomly selected avatars.
- Both sides use 100 OVR equalized players and the mirrored Master Tactician coaches. This removes rating variance while testing arbitrary avatar interactions. The random assignment is deterministic for its displayed match seed; selecting `New Random 5v5` in the arena creates a fresh seed and matchup.
- Developer matches do not award Clash Coins, evolution progress, tournament wins/losses, or ladder rating. The entry card is guarded by `import.meta.env.DEV` and is absent from production builds.
- Random means random rather than role-balanced, so unusual all-carry or low-frontline compositions are valid test cases. All normal combat, objective, tower-dive, pathing, and attribution invariants remain active.
- `src/devRandomMode.test.mjs` checks same-seed reproducibility, different-seed rerolls, five players per side, uniqueness across all ten player/avatar slots, 100 OVR normalization, and undersized-pool rejection. `npm run check:game` passed design validation, all 184 game tests, all 3 multi-seed simulation tests, and TypeScript. `npm run build` passed with the existing large-bundle advisory; `git diff --check` passed.

## Enlarged Realistic Battlefield Models

- The live Clash Arena uses dedicated procedural battlefield model renderers for the Nexus, towers, all three barracks classes, and melee, caster, and cannon/catapult minions. Central scale metrics preserve a clear hierarchy: the Nexus is the largest landmark, towers dominate barracks, and cannon minions are the largest ordinary wave unit.
- Nexus models now use a broad contact shadow, stepped stone-and-metal foundation, four support pylons, rotating machinery rings, six faceted orbiting emitters, a multi-facet illuminated core, and animated seal runes while invulnerable.
- Towers now use a widened foundation, tapered masonry shaft, structural buttresses, team banners, battlements, and a housed floating crystal. Their live targeting beam originates from the enlarged crystal crown.
- Melee, ranged, and catapult barracks retain their gameplay identities while gaining masonry courses, dimensional foundations, team lighting, and more complete forge, watchtower, bow, wheels, siege frame, throwing arm, and ammunition details. Destroyed buildings leave larger scattered rubble fields instead of a single flat rectangle.
- Ordinary melee minions use compact articulated wardling bodies, faceted sallet helmets, cyclops visors, angular shields, and short polearms. Casters use masked angular hoods, split robes, crescent staffs, and glowing spell focuses. Cannon waves use compact wheeled arc-launcher catapults with a shielded chassis, throwing arm, loaded energy ammunition, and a tiny ember operator. These silhouettes keep familiar lane-role readability while remaining original to Esports Clash.
- This is a presentation-only scale change. Structure positions, unit collision radii, tower and Nexus attack ranges, projectile rules, health, damage, minion stats, pathing, targeting, barracks upgrade behavior, and protected tower-dive rules are unchanged.
- `src/battlefieldModels.test.mjs` checks the visual scale hierarchy and smoke-renders every structure, barracks, minion, team, and sealed/unsealed Nexus variant. `npm run check:game` passed design validation, all 186 game tests, all 3 multi-seed simulation tests, and TypeScript. `npm run build` passed with the existing large-bundle advisory; `git diff --check` passed.
- Ordinary minion render scales were reduced from 1.52-1.68 to 0.98-1.14, with matching smaller shadows, empowerment rings, and health bars. Their simulation collision radii, attack ranges, stats, and target selection did not change.
- Decorative curved camp roads and dashed center markings were removed from the terrain renderer. Camp approach routing and rock-collision waypoints are still active but are no longer painted on the ground.
- Limit: these remain stylized procedural 2D canvas models rather than textured 3D meshes. A live browser pass is still useful to tune visual crowding during unusually dense waves.

## Terrain Routing and Defensive Calls (Wider Battlefield)

- `arenaRules.ts` supplies shared scalloped wall contours to collision stones and the layered canvas cliff renderer. Openings remain lane-facing and mirrored; center boss contours are themselves symmetric. No walking-speed boost, ghosting through stone, or visible route guide is added.
- `terrainRouting.ts` caches body-expanded static graphs and per-unit routes, uses A* and collision-checked segment smoothing, and replans after goal changes, displacement, or stalled progress. Camp farming, camp exit to lane, normal recovery, base reinforcement, and neutral chase/reset use these waypoints. Collision repairs embedded stationary bodies, but normal failed steps fall back to the previous walkable point rather than jumping across a wall. Upper outer buff centers are farther outward to avoid sealed top-boundary pockets.
- `baseDefenseRules.ts` ranks threatened Nexus > tier 3 > barracks > inner tower > outer tower. Scouted living champions and approaching enemy creeps trigger calls; destroyed structures and absent/dead threats do not. Inner-base calls recruit any available healthy defender across the map; ordinary tower calls recruit within 650 units. Units rally 65 units behind the building, target attackers through the standard combat pipeline, and clear approaching waves.
- Defense preempts farming, new epic-objective starts, post-objective staging, optional shopping resets, and enemy-Nexus pushes. Healthy nearby recallers can interrupt their channel; distant recalls complete to arrive home faster. Natural retreat, low-health recovery, existing skill channels, risk-based tower commitments, and turret-safe positioning retain precedence. No defensive damage/HP buffs or suicide overrides are granted.
- Affected code: `arenaLayout.ts`, `arenaRules.ts`, `terrainRouting.ts`, `baseDefenseRules.ts`, `neutralAggroRules.ts`, `macroFarmRules.ts`, live `AramMatchView.tsx`, and headless layout initialization. Tests cover mirrored priorities, dead threats, 90 camp rotations, 120 well/flank round trips, compass approaches, large-monster gates, and embedded/stalled recovery. Required final verification is `npm run check:game` plus `npm run build` (results in the handoff).
- Limits: headless combat does not model the full live defensive/recall state machine; no browser replay or competitive-balance claim. Existing dynamic chase steering still relies on collision sliding rather than globally planned combat paths.

## Ability Sound Effects Library and Regression Verification

- `public/audio/abilities/` contains the complete library of 135 generated ability audio files (45 playable avatars $\times$ 3 abilities each: `skill1`, `skill2`, and `ultimate`).
- Each audio file is formatted as mono 48 kHz 24-bit PCM with true-peak headroom $\le -2.90$ dBFS to prevent clipping or distortion during intense teamfights.
- Ability cues adhere to slot duration standards: Skill 1 (0.45â€“0.90s), Skill 2 (0.55â€“1.10s), and Ultimate (1.20â€“2.20s).
- `public/audio/abilities/manifest.csv` records the exact metadata (`avatar`, `slot`, `ability`, `filename`, `duration_seconds`, `sample_rate`, `bit_depth`, `channels`, `peak_dbfs`) for all 135 assets.
- In `src/audio.ts`, `SoundManager` loads the manifest, maps cues dynamically to their recorded WAV file, applies stereo panning based on horizontal arena coordinate (`pan = x / ARENA_WIDTH * 2 - 1`), routes audio through the combat mix bus, and preserves designed reverberation tails.
- `SoundManager.preloadAvatarSkills(avatarNames)` preloads and decodes drafted champions' ability audio into memory upon match start in `src/components/AramMatchView.tsx`, preventing audio hitching or delay on initial cast.
- In case a file is unreadable on an unsupported client, the runtime cleanly falls back to legacy licensed samples and procedural layer synthesis (`playLegacyCue`), ensuring audio continuity.
- `src/avatarSkillAudio.test.mjs` enforces 1:1 manifest-to-disk parity, validates header bytes for 48kHz 24-bit mono PCM, verifies true-peak headroom, and ensures no cues are missing or mismatched.

## Highground Base Scouting, Base Rock Pathing, and Low Nexus Commitment

- **Highground Base Scouting:** Approaching champions (within 300px of enemy highground base ramps) proactively place highground scout wards (`ðŸ‘ï¸ HIGHGROUND WARD`) into unwarded highground flank bushes (`bush_red_high_north`, `bush_red_high_south` or `bush_blue_high_north`, `bush_blue_high_south`) or on the base entrance ramp ($x = 1930$ / $670$). These wards carve dynamic vision holes through the highground fog of war and reveal enemy defenders waiting in ambush.
- **Retreat Pathing Safety & Anti-Trap Filtering:** Retreating units filter out enemy base ramp bushes (`!isEnemyBaseRampBush`). Units retreating while in enemy territory route along the open central lane corridor ($y = LANE_Y = 380$) toward their allied fountain well, eliminating collisions with flanking highground rocks and preventing units from getting trapped against enemy base rock faces.
- **Low-Health Nexus Commitment:** When the enemy Nexus is exposed (`canDamageNexus` is true) and critically low ($\le 40\%$ HP), attackers prioritize destroying the Nexus (`âš”ï¸ NEXUS COMMIT!`). Attackers commit even with defenders alive nearby (overriding conservative 16% HP defender standoff holds), relax minimum health down to 18% (or lower on critical nexus), and bypass turret perimeter tether halts. Headless simulation engine in `src/matchSimulationEngine.ts` dynamically evaluates `exposedNexus` in `laneAdvanceLimit` to ensure simulated matches advance cleanly.

## Battlefield Zoom, Spectator Action Camera, and Mini Radar Map

- **Spectator Zoom & Viewport Clamping:** To resolve the visual shrinking caused by expanding the arena world width to 2600 units within fixed-ratio displays, Clash Arena features a spectator camera with a default `1.45x` zoom (scaling visible world width down from 2600 to ~1793 units). Champion avatars, models, projectiles, and floating indicators render ~45% larger and easily readable. Presets include `Fit` (1.0x, full arena width), `1.45x` (balanced action), and `1.85x` (close-up combat), with custom zoom from 1.0x to 2.5x.
- **Auto Action Camera:** Evaluates alive champions with dynamic combat weights (base 1.0, low HP <60% +1.5, crowd controlled or channeling +2.0, and active engagements with enemies within 520px +3.5). The camera smoothly glides to the action center with exponential delta-time damping (`1 - exp(-4.2 * dt)`), tethered to lane ($y = 380$) while tilting vertically toward Dragon ($y \approx 160$) or Golem ($y \approx 600$) during pit engagements.
- **Strict Boundary Clamping:** Camera viewport is clamped to `[halfW, ARENA_WIDTH - halfW]` horizontally and `[halfH, 760 - halfH]` vertically. At all zoom levels, out-of-bounds void or black borders are never displayed. At 1.0x (Fit), the viewport locks strictly to the full arena bounds $(1300, 380)$.
- **Screen-Space Mini Radar Map:** Renders a 260x76px radar at 1:10 scale in the bottom-right corner showing live towers, boss markers, champion dots, and an interactive golden camera viewport rectangle. Clicking or dragging on the minimap instantly jumps or scrolls the camera view across the battlefield. Toggled with key `M` or the on-screen button.
- **Interactive Spectator Controls:** A single compact toolbar provides Auto/Free camera mode, fit/action zoom, and `+`/`-` buttons. Canvas supports drag-to-pan, mouse wheel zooming, touch pinch/drag, and `Spacebar` / double-click to re-center. Keyboard shortcuts do not override focused buttons, inputs, or other interactive controls.
- Simulation coordinates ($[0, 2600] \times [0, 760]$), physics, pathing, and deterministic seed progression are unchanged.

## Three.js presentation boundary (live 3D foundation, 2026-10-17)

- The user requested a 3D simulation view inspired by Wild Rift. This expands the historical Phase 1 underlay-only scope. `AramMatchView.tsx` remains the sole live fixed-step simulator; do not mount the unused independent `ThreeAramArena.tsx` simulator.
- The default view uses `threeArenaScene.ts`: lit procedural mesh actors, structures, monsters, summons, wards, real rock/bush layouts, decorative edge trees, wells and generic combat effects over the shader ground. Static props are instanced; actor geometry/materials and transient effect meshes are reused. The ground plane maps simulation X/Y to Three X/Z, with visual height on Y.
- One existing RAF drives simulation and presentation. Three must never schedule simulation, mutate world inputs, consume gameplay RNG, resolve collisions/hits/damage, or make animation completion a combat clock. Walking/attack/recall/control poses read simulation state/time. Logical terrain, height-independent combat and 2600x760 coordinates remain authoritative.
- The tilted orthographic camera uses 1.0-2.5 zoom, default 2.0, ground-plane drag/touch math, automatic action follow and minimap navigation. Portrait screens crop horizontally while preserving vertical viewing scale. Camera changes affect presentation only; projected HUD labels use CSS pixels and minimap hit bounds match its responsive display.
- The transparent Canvas overlay owns projected health/mana, aliases, camp labels, combat text and minimap in 3D. DOM/React owns conventional controls, roster, logs and modals. The 2D tactical toggle retains the complete existing Canvas rendering and the flat Three ground underlay; no healthy view double-paints ground/actors.
- Arena-only lazy loading, maximum 2x DPR, `ResizeObserver`, context-loss Canvas fallback/restoration and complete resource/listener/observer disposal remain mandatory. Disposal includes instance GPU buffers and cached/pooled resources even when their last visible actor has gone.
- Protected current rules remain risk-based tower/well/Nexus combat, real damage/range/targeting, normal terrain-aware retreat, safe bushes/perimeter, 10-second kill attribution, champion-first objective contests above 32%, fixed-step determinism, anti-oscillation and parody identities. Historical mandatory abort/forced evacuation/+35 sprint remain superseded.
- This is a procedural 3D foundation: unique authored models/rigs/textures, detailed per-avatar transformations/channels/telegraphs and Canvas atmospheric fog are not yet ported. Actual visibility, targeting and spell mechanics still use the same simulator. Keep 2D available for richer tactical visual detail. No full-match/device-performance or art-complete claim follows.
- Verification: 366 game tests, three deterministic simulations, TypeScript and build pass (existing chunk advisory). Frozen-input/camera/disposal tests and live real-context recovery pass. Seed-47 captured world/RNG and execution/opportunity audits match 3D versus 2D at 30/60/90 seconds. Mouse/touch pan, responsive minimap, three 3D layouts and the existing five-viewport spectator/control smoke pass; phone toolbar wrap is corrected. See `arena-3d-migration.md`, `arena-3d-verification.json` and screenshots. The historical ML source freeze is preserved; these renderer changes do not deploy or improve the failed offline candidate.

## Context-sensitive retreat routing (Phase 2, 2026-10-11)

- A retreat is a destination-and-route decision, not an automatic nearby-bush selection. Candidate destinations are homeward lane ground, allied tower cover, healthy teammates moving homeward, safe open ground, fountain, and safe bushes.
- Candidate safety is a hard gate before card preferences: the point must be passable, reachable by the shared terrain graph, at least 50px beyond every live enemy structure's range, and at least 150px from an enemy (220px for critical-health avatars). A valid safe bush is optional cover, never a safety bypass.
- IQ and TF affect only the ordering of already safe candidates. IQ values safe homeward progress and close cover under immediate pressure; TF values regrouping at healthy allies and allied towers. They cannot select a bush in turret range, an enemy-adjacent point, an impassable destination, or an unreachable route.
- The live unit holds a valid retreat plan for approximately 0.65-0.95 seconds to prevent route switching. It may replan immediately for a material safety change: enemy arrival, enemy-tower danger, or impassable terrain. This supplements and does not replace the protected 0.45s retreat/fight decision commitment or 12px facing deadband.
- Normal retreat movement continues to use `rockApproachWaypoint` and collision-safe terrain movement. The same terrain graph provides a route-length estimate for selection; no independent routing system exists.
- Being inside enemy turret range prevents an idle stop and can invalidate an unsafe destination, but it does not override the planner with a fixed vector or special speed. The ordinary terrain-aware route remains in control at normal movement speed.
- Plans are cleared upon resuming normal activity. No unit may intentionally idle at a retreat destination inside enemy turret range.

## Retreat movement speed (Phase 3, 2026-10-11)

- Ordinary live retreat uses the normal terrain-routing base speed of 85 units/s. Retreat state alone grants no movement-speed bonus.
- Retreat speed is calculated from an explicit additive breakdown: 85 ordinary base, Pathfinder Boots, Kaelen wind-orb movement, active stealth movement, and the nearby Windwalker's Warhorn +20 aura. These are item/ability effects, not retreat bonuses.
- Failed-dive evacuation and any unit still inside enemy turret range use the established 105 emergency base. The former +35 rescue sprint was removed after explicit user confirmation on 2026-10-12, reducing the unmodified emergency total from 140 to 105 units/s. Boots and legitimate ability/item modifiers still apply.
- Paxi orb relocation and Raijin's 540-unit/s Ball Lightning are discrete escape abilities rather than ordinary retreat speed. Their cooldown, mana, targeting, and safety rules remain authoritative.
- Playback speed changes wall-clock presentation of the whole fixed-step simulation; it must never be read as an actor movement modifier.
- `movementSpeedRules.ts` owns the live retreat-speed breakdown. The headless engine remains simplified and does not represent live retreat routing or speed balance.

## Basic-attack reach and structure safety (Phase 4, 2026-10-11)

- `basicAttackRangeRules.ts` is the authoritative source for avatar basic-attack weapon reach in both the live and headless simulators. All playable avatars must resolve to a finite 40-205 unit range. Existing authored melee/ranged differences and Skybreaker's fixed 180-unit Propella range remain; card IQ, TF, OVR, and playstyle must never change physical attack range.
- Structure contact uses explicit target footprints: tower 32, barracks 35, and Nexus 58 units. Effective center-to-center reach is basic weapon range plus that footprint. Because towers and the Nexus retaliate, their basic-attack reach is capped at their own targeting radii: 135 and 190 units respectively. Barracks do not attack and retain edge-aware uncapped reach.
- The live simulator must revalidate structure existence and range when an attack windup fires. Selection-time range alone cannot authorize a hit after either side moves. The headless simulator uses the same predicate rather than a kit-range approximation.
- The turret no-wave perimeter remains 28 units beyond the 135-unit tower radius, at 163 units from center. An attacker held there cannot basic-attack the tower. Once within 135 units, existing turret targeting and minion/champion aggro rules remain authoritative.
- Basic attacks and abilities are separate range systems. `getSkillCastRange` continues to own spells, including legitimate long-range casts; tower safety changes must not globally shorten abilities. No runtime temporary basic-range modifier currently exists. Kindra's described +15% range and Buck's described Smoke Screen range limit remain an explicit Phase 7 ability-consistency gap.
- Tower-dive authorization is unchanged: class, HP/raw HP, minion buffer, IQ, lethal/execute checks, and outnumbering gates still apply. Abort conditions, attack suppression, evacuation vector, and the protected +35 evacuation sprint remain intact.
- Verification: the focused Phase 4 suites passed 34/34; full `npm run check:game` passed 253 game tests, 3 multi-seed simulations, design validation, and TypeScript; `npm run build` passed. No live browser balance or frame-time claim was made.

## Concrete action commitment and recovery (Phase 5, 2026-10-12)

- `decisionActionRecovery.ts` owns concrete action/target retention and failure retry state. It supplements rather than replaces `decisionCommitment.ts`: the latter remains authoritative for the protected 0.45-second retreat and 0.35-second fight states.
- Concrete actions cover champion, structure, minion, camp, objective, defense, regroup, stage, relic, push, and hold. Default commitment is 0.75 seconds; explicitly coordinated objective/defense actions use 0.9-1.0 seconds. Three switches within 2.4 seconds trigger a minimum 1.15-second commitment. Existing target scoring and +1.4 stickiness remain in force.
- Failures are action-and-target specific: unavailable retries after 0.65 seconds, unsafe after 1.6, unreachable after 2.4, and non-progressing after 1.35. Up to 12 recent failures remain inspectable for eight seconds. They block only while their retry time and context both still match.
- Context changes release a block immediately: target movement, allied/enemy wave-count changes, local ally/enemy-count changes, and ability readiness are material. This permits reconsideration when a wave arrives, danger recedes, a route/target changes, or an ability becomes available.
- Moving at least four units or gaining four units toward the target refreshes progress. Reachability is checked after 0.45 seconds without progress; 0.9 seconds without progress records a stall and releases the action. Attack/cast execution and deliberate safe holds are not movement stalls.
- Each selection pass examines at most 12 candidates in existing deterministic order and consumes no gameplay RNG. Retry-blocked champion, camp, minion, and structure targets yield to existing viable alternatives. If none is productive, the unit uses the existing lane advance limit and explicitly holds idle on arrival rather than walking in place.
- Enemy-tower chase remains a hard safety boundary. An unauthorized champion chase is recorded unsafe and converts to `getTurretPerimeterHoldPoint`, still 28 units outside tower range. An arriving allied wave changes context and permits the normal dive rules to reevaluate; the action layer cannot authorize a dive.
- Death, recall, crowd control, committed retreat, and dive abort are emergency/state interrupts. They clear concrete intent without creating retry penalties. Dive class/HP/minion/IQ/execute/outnumbering rules, failed-dive evacuation and exact +35 sprint, turret-safe retreats, champion-first objective contest above 32%, facing deadband, and target stickiness are unchanged.
- Runtime state lives on `AramChampionUnit.actionIntent` and `actionFailures`. The simplified headless engine does not run this live policy. Verification passed 37/37 focused tests, 261 game tests, 3 deterministic simulation tests, TypeScript, and the production build; no live browser effectiveness or performance measurement was taken.

## Teamfight-First Tempo Rework (2026-10-12)

- Tempo remains a quiet-period macro policy, not a reason to delay viable local combat. `teamTempoRules.ts` recognizes an available, visible, targetable champion within `min(400, max(190, basicAttackRange * 1.5, readyCastRange))`, or an existing eligible allied fight to reinforce. This is an opportunity radius only: physical attacks, spell ranges, movement speeds, and damage timing do not change. Existing card-driven target selection and TF >= 55 reinforcement eligibility remain authoritative.
- Ordinary fight priority cannot override low-health recovery, dangerous-fight/retreat commitment, dive aborts, or turret fire. The separate decisive channel-follow-up policy retains its established low-health exception. A direct approach to a target or reinforcement anchor must avoid every live enemy tower/Nexus range plus the 28-unit perimeter buffer, including crossings with safe endpoints. This uses existing segment/circle geometry, not a new pathfinder or dive authorization rule.
- Viable fights suppress optional wave-led/recent-kill push calls, relic detours, shopping/resource resets, fresh neutral starts, camp farming, pre-objective staging, and post-secure regrouping. Ordinary wave clearing yields to combat or reinforcement. The wave chase leash may yield only to the selected locally viable champion, not merely because an unrelated ally needs help. Quiet-lane advance limits and unsupported/tower-protected chase restrictions remain.
- Coordinated objective recall plans now expire when the existing team-threat check becomes true, instead of persisting through renewed pressure. Channels already in progress keep their existing interruption rules. Critical relic recovery, threatened-base defense, exposed/low-Nexus finish behavior, and the contested boss <=32% finish exception remain unchanged.
- Affected files: `src/teamTempoRules.ts`, `src/teamTempoRules.test.mjs`, `src/components/AramMatchView.tsx`, and both handoff/mechanics documents. Verification: 41 focused tests passed (including five new tempo cases), `npm run check:game` passed, and `npm run build` passed with the existing large-chunk advisory. No rendering/UI changes or browser check; no live-match fight-frequency, balance, or frame-time improvement has been measured. Terrain detours still use existing movement handling. Phase 6 remains unstarted.

## Phase 6: Creep Attack Timing and Selective Wave-Clear Skills (2026-10-12)

- Avatar basic attacks against lane creeps, ordinary camps, and epic monsters now have one bounded `creepAttack` state. Windup is `min(0.24, attackPeriod * 0.28)` seconds; recovery ends at `min(attackPeriod * 0.8, windup + 0.2)` after attack start. The existing attack cooldown still starts at selection. Melee damage and ranged launch occur once at release, after that actor's safety/decision pass, in roster order. Champion-vs-champion attacks, structure attacks, and outgoing minion/camp attacks keep their existing timing.
- Release rechecks target life and actual range. Death, recall, retreat commitment, dive abort, stasis/control/channeling, another cast, or a changed action target cancels a pending strike without refunding its cooldown. Rendering only reads simulation timestamps: it cannot launch projectiles or deal damage. Attack speed changes do not retime an already-started pose. Attack RNG is sampled on release, so old-version live replay outcomes are not promised; the new fixed-step behavior remains seeded and independent of render speed.
- Ranged creep/camp attacks track their living intended target and clamp travel to the arrival point; dead targets discard the shot. Damage happens only on arrival. Existing projectile blocking remains in force. Buck's authored multi-pellet attack and Runaan secondary shots are retained. Non-creep projectiles are not retimed by this increment. Surviving creeps show a 0.16-second simulation-time hit ring; camps retain their existing damage-driven hit flash and leash/reset rules.
- The existing wave-clear branch and `shouldCastWaveClearSkill` now evaluate actual Skill 1 mana cost, full rank/haste-adjusted cooldown (removing the old farming-only 0.8 discount), a reserve of `maxMana * (0.2 + IQ * 0.001 + TF * 0.001)`, at least two creeps needing more than one base basic hit, kill potential/useful damage, and siege opportunity. Enemies within 450 units or an objective spawning within cooldown + 4 seconds block the cast. LAN/IQ, Laning Demon, and Objective Macro coaching affect preference, never bypass these gates. Teamfight-first tempo and emergency recovery remain above optional wave clear. No ultimate is spent for this task.
- Explicit Skill 1 wave delivery profiles replace the fabricated instant 90-unit AoE for every kit: Soulscourge is a 75-unit ground burst; Stonewake uses the existing target-centered rectangle (half-width 90, half-height 32); Nullweaver, Oathmute, Corsara, Brewmaw, Hweilin, Jaxon, and Skybreaker reuse their existing first-skill projectile pipeline with splash radii 42/72/85/90/75/60/105 respectively. Wave projectiles collide with the first enemy champion or minion along their path, ignore allies, and do not target camps/structures. Primary minion damage resolves once and other minions in the actual impact splash take 55%. Existing champion impact handling remains authoritative if a champion intercepts. Area indicators show the actual circle/rectangle at impact, not at launch.
- Wave damage uses ranked Skill 1 base plus existing capped item scaling, retaining the established `(0.7 + LAN / 180)` wave factor. Unsupported deliveries (including hooks, buffs, toggles, dashes and Conflux) fall back to basics; extending the remaining kits belongs to an explicit ability audit rather than granting them invented target types. This is not all-avatar ability parity.
- Files: `src/creepAttackTiming.ts` and tests, `src/waveClearRules.ts` and tests, `src/types.ts`, `src/components/AramMatchView.tsx`, `src/components/ChampionSpriteRenderer.ts`, `scripts/phase6-browser-fixture.mjs`, and the existing spectator browser checker. Verification: 33 focused tests, all 276 game tests plus 3 simulation tests, TypeScript, and production build passed. Controlled live browser checks observed 0.24-second windups, exactly one 82-damage melee hit and one 75-damage ranged hit (ranged checked at 1x and 4x), plus a single projectile wave impact on three creeps. The five viewport smoke checks and pose screenshot review passed. These are controlled fixtures, not full-match balance/performance measurements. Phase 7 remains unstarted.

## Compact One-Screen Spectator Shell

- `AramMatchView.tsx` now occupies the viewport while a live match is mounted, without browser-page scrolling. `spectator.css` allocates a compact score header, unified playback/camera toolbar, announcement/objective rail, flexible undistorted canvas, and bottom broadcast dock. This replaces the previous stacked match panels, not the manager/draft pages. Exiting to Lobby restores the original page overflow state.
- `SpectatorRoster.tsx` shows both five-player teams simultaneously with HP/shield/mana/XP, level or respawn timer, KDA, CS, gold, ability readiness, item/boots inspection, and ward readiness. Arsenaldo shows invoked D/F spells instead of a false generic ultimate indicator. Portrait buttons focus the existing free camera.
- Event log and power-spike tabs paginate inside the same fixed dock; they do not extend the page. Match insights and item detail overlays remain available on demand, with scrolling allowed for long details. Replay, balance batches, exports, coaches, seed, and music remain under More. Kill/announcement content stays outside the battlefield.
- `scripts/check-spectator-browser.mjs` tests an isolated real arena and spellbook through local Chrome DevTools. Verified widths/heights: 1920x1080, 1366x768, 1280x720, 1024x600, and 390x844. Controls, ten roster rows, unclipped inventory bounds, pause, tabs, item details, insights, exit restoration, ten spellbook selectors, and live Arsenaldo invocations are checked. Run with a disposable debug browser on port 9223 and Vite on 5173; `CHECK_COMBAT=1` adds the live cast check.
- A portrait phone fits the interface but its arena is smaller; desktop/landscape is preferred for watching fights. The canvas uses its actual rendered bounds for camera/minimap input, preserving aspect ratio without letterbox-coordinate errors. Required `npm run check:game` and `npm run build` results are recorded in the handoff; the bundle-size advisory remains.

Falls back to direct drawing when no DOM canvas exists (node tests).

## Ability consistency rules (Phase 7, 2026-10-12)

- Every playable kit must retain complete passive/active descriptions, valid finite damage/cooldown values, one established cast type per active, and a finite cast range. The roster-wide audit currently covers 45 avatars and 135 active abilities. Ability item ratios remain capped at 10%; card stats may influence decisions but never silently alter physical ranges or spell definitions.
- Kindra's Spirit Mark is the explicit exception to static base weapon reach. A basic hit on a jungle camp, dragon, or champion already at or below 40% HP grants a five-second, refresh-only +15% basic-attack range and damage buff. It never stacks. Her authored 185 range becomes 212.75 while active. Skill ranges remain fixed at 185/185/259, and armed-structure retaliation caps remain authoritative.
- Kindra's Sanctuary of Eternity is a self-centered 65-radius zone lasting four seconds. Any champion insideâ€”ally or enemyâ€”cannot fall below 10% maximum HP. There are no damage ticks. When that individual zone expires once, living allies still inside heal for 450 base HP multiplied by the existing ultimate rank multiplier; enemies and allies outside do not heal. The cast uses the normal 100 ultimate mana and rank-adjusted cooldown path.
- Buck's Smoke Screen is a point cast with 110 range. On cast it deals one 60-base magic area hit, using existing +10% bonus AD/+6% bonus AP and rank scaling, to enemy champions in its 55-radius area. The cloud lasts 2.5 seconds and caps an enemy inside to 55 basic-attack range. It never changes spell range, applies no stun/root, has no damage ticks, does not stack its range cap, and ends immediately for a unit that leaves. It costs 35 mana and uses its 10-second base cooldown with existing rank/haste adjustment.
- Buck's basic attack displays exactly four pellets, each carrying one quarter of the already-resolved attack damage. The pellets represent one attack, not four full attacks or a permanent armor stack. The prior unsupported +15 armor tooltip claim is removed.
- Arsenaldo's Conflux remains the authoritative ten-spell system in `kaelenAbilities.ts` and `kaelenSpellRuntime.ts`: three-orb FIFO recipes, two invoked FIFO slots, spell-specific mana/cooldown/range/shape/delay/speed/damage/control, swept line collision with once-per-target hit IDs, once-only delayed bursts, and hit-gated AI combos. Do not add a second invocation or damage path.
- The shared `abilityRuntimeRules.ts` constants and helpers are the authoritative contract for the new Kindra/Buck mechanics. Rendering reads these simulation states and effects; it does not grant buffs, apply Smoke damage, heal Respite, or determine expiry.
- Verification: focused Phase 7 tests passed 3/3; `npm run check:game` passed 279 game tests plus 3 deterministic simulation tests and TypeScript; production build and five-viewport browser checks passed. The controlled browser values were 212.75 hunted range, 55 smoked range, 100 HP floor per 1000 max HP, and 450 base expiry heal. The headless simulator does not model these live mechanics and cannot be used as Phase 7 balance evidence.

## Proactive teamfight all-ins and failed-dive punishment (pre-Phase 8, 2026-10-12)

- A team no longer needs landed crowd control or recent champion damage to begin coordinated combat. `shouldStartProactiveAllIn` permits a visible target within 500 units when at least three allies and three enemies are grouped, allies are not outnumbered, the initiating avatar has at least 50% HP, allied average HP is at least 56%, team health is not more than ten percentage points behind, and at least 60% of grouped allies have a basic ability or ultimate ready. IQ and TF affect the call score; a balanced 4v4/5v5 gets an explicit full-clash allowance.
- Tower safety is absolute for this call: an unsafe approach, target under an enemy tower/Nexus perimeter, current turret fire, or dive abort rejects it. The policy grants no damage, range, cooldown, or movement-speed bonus and consumes no RNG.
- Once called, the team commits for up to 12 simulation seconds instead of reconsidering immediately after cooldowns are spent or the first teammate dies. Individual commitment ends below 25% HP, on turret fire/dive abort, when the target is tower-protected, when the approach becomes unsafe, or when the timer expires. This is intentionally stronger than the ordinary 0.35-second fight commitment but does not override protected turret rules.
- During the all-in, allies deterministically focus the safe visible enemy with the lowest HP plus shield, then lowest HP fraction and stable ID. Concrete champion intent remains committed for 1.2 seconds, preserving Phase 5 failure handling and normal target stickiness outside the call. After a won fight creates the existing `shouldPressWonFight` window, healthy advantaged allies may use the same 500-unit safe finish leash against survivors.
- Failed-dive abort conditions, outward evacuation vector, attack suppression, abort cooldown, and clearance requirement remain. Per explicit user confirmation, `movementSpeedRules.ts` no longer adds the former +35 evacuation sprint; the unmodified live evacuation pace is 105 instead of 140 units/s, so failed dives receive more punishment.
- Files: `src/teamTempoRules.ts`, `src/teamTempoRules.test.mjs`, `src/movementSpeedRules.ts`, `src/movementSpeedRules.test.mjs`, `src/types.ts`, `src/components/AramMatchView.tsx`, `scripts/combat-engage-browser-fixture.mjs`, and `scripts/check-spectator-browser.mjs`. Focused tests passed 24/24; `npm run check:game` passed 281 game tests, 3 deterministic simulation tests, design validation, and TypeScript; production build passed. Controlled live verification began with ten of ten units accepting champion intents without any active channel/landed CC. The 30-simulation-second diagnostic observed three total deaths and a minimum of three red survivors in its final run; it did not observe a complete team wipe, so wipe frequency in representative matches remains unmeasured.

## Tower durability and siege scaling (Phase 8, 2026-10-12)

- Structure health is outer 3,600, inner 5,000, nexus tower 6,500, barracks 3,000, and Nexus 9,000. Inner-base durability rises without regeneration, an artificial time gate, or invented armor.
- Champion building basics use authored AD including purchased AD plus persistent buff AD. Crit bonus, Kraken/BORK, Kindra hunt, overcharge, lifesteal, and Navori do not trigger on structures. The crit RNG draw remains solely to preserve deterministic consumption order.
- No allied minion keeps the 0.28 backdoor factor; one removes it. Extra minions add 5% each, a cannon adds 10%, capped at 1.35. Minions retain class siege damage. Dragon Aspect retains 1.5 champion siege for its owner for 120 seconds in live and headless; Gravemarch's 1,050 plated charge is unchanged.
- Plating remains 30% initially and reaches 100% at eight minutes. Sequential progression, Nexus shielding, turret rules, dive limits/abort without the removed +35 bonus, attribution, and base defense remain authoritative.
- Representative full-team/full-wave TTD after the change is outer/inner/nexus-tower 10.6/14.7/19.1 seconds at 120s, 3.4/4.8/6.2 at 300s, and 1.5/2.1/2.7 at 540s. These rule-level estimates exclude defenders, travel, Dragon and Gravemarch.
- Verification: 25 focused tests, 285 game tests, 3 deterministic simulations, TypeScript, and build passed. The same ten headless seeds averaged 8.5 minutes before and 8.3 after; all post-change matches ended within 5.0â€“12.0 minutes. Rendering/UI did not change, so no browser visual check was performed.
