- Use existing responsibilities where possible for `OBSERVE â†’ EVALUATE â†’ PREPARE â†’ ENGAGE â†’ REASSESS â†’ RESOLVE â†’ TRANSITION`, explainable deterministic utilities, meaningful triggers, role autonomy, non-omniscient perception, diagnostics, and clean resumption of assignments.
- Cover the attachment's 30 minimum scenario regressions. Preserve tower-dive/abort, retreat, attribution, objective, commitment, RNG-order, bounded-pass, farming, recall, defense and alias invariants. Outcomes must emerge from combat, not scripts.

## Personality psychology invariants (2026-10-12)

- Personality is a decision preference layer only. Primary/secondary personality, temporary mood, teammate relationships, and derived team psychology may change utilities for legal engage, chase, peel, objective, siege, wave, regroup, farm, and retreat choices; they never change champion damage, HP, defenses, movement speed, attack/range, cooldowns, healing, shields, or objective execute damage.
- Personality is evaluated before existing safety and commitment enforcement. It cannot authorize a tower dive, cross a turret perimeter, override an abort/retreat, break contested-objective champion-first behavior above 32% HP, reveal unseen enemies, bypass recall/base rules, or consume new RNG. Mood events are deduplicated and recover by simulation time.
- The live observed-event set is champion kill/death/assist, nearby multi-player fight resolution, full wipe, objective secure/loss, structure siege resolution, defended-structure kill, and an observed failed group engagement. The latter requires two recently damaged players per side in close contact, a 3.4-second separation, no participant death, meaningful participant health loss, and a documented retreat toward home; it cannot infer blame from a single trade, movement, or unseen enemy. A participant death ends this detector and creates an eight-second suppression window, leaving actual kill/teamfight resolution authoritative. Team relationships update only for these bounded five-player events. Objective/IGL preferences may decline an otherwise legal optional start or shape an existing post-secure plan, but never make an illegal objective, tower, recall, or retreat decision legal.
- Decision telemetry is observational. For each selected action it records the existing-policy baseline, separate personality/mood/coordination contributions, IGL alignment, target and commitment when applicable, plus the safety/route context. An abort, retreat, or recall records the protective reason that superseded aggression. Telemetry cannot feed back into action selection, alter RNG, or bypass safety.
- Recovery/social events must be provable. Multikill mood changes use actual staged kill records; a comeback requires a real nearby group fight win while at least two kills or 1,000 net-worth behind; a rescue requires a concrete ally heal/shield while the recipient is low HP and recently hit by an identified enemy. Pair relationship credit is event-deduplicated. IGL leadership respect changes only when an active IGL signal has a real objective/teamfight outcome. No generic damage, ordinary sustain, or guessed teammate mistake changes social state.

## kovi card invariant (2026-10-12)

- `p_kovi` is one owned-by-default Diamond Mage reserve with permanent Clutch Performer + Showman personalities. His high-pressure three-target area-ultimate preference is a legal-decision preference only: it does not alter damage, health, defenses, movement, ability definitions, range, cooldown, targetability, objectives, or protected tower/retreat/commitment behavior. Roster reconciliation may add this ID once and remove only duplicate copies of this ID.

## Personality combination and call-response invariants (2026-10-12)

- Personality pairs are conditional decision directives, not additive card buffs. IGL + Untiltable supports recovery calls only after actual setbacks; Bodyguard + Loyalist prefers legal peel for a threatened ally; Objective Zealot + Steal Gambler fights the opposing champions while a contested boss exceeds 32% and may favor the already-authorized finish at or below 32%; other pair effects require their documented health, numbers, visibility, safe-route, resource, or recent-outcome conditions.
- Overconfident + Survivalist produces one coherent risk preference per decision. Retreat/regroup wins whenever the unit is low, outnumbered, or unsafe. Engage/chase pressure can exist only while the unit is healthy, supported, and safe. The existing commitment timer, turret checks, dive abort, recall rules, and emergency exits remain final authority.
- Personality-aware IGL alignment can modestly change whether a player follows an otherwise legal call; it cannot make a poor call safe. Challenger disagreement is limited to adverse observed circumstances, Egoistic deviation requires a real isolated opportunity, and no response reveals hidden enemies or bypasses protected route/objective conditions.
- Pair names and deltas are decision telemetry only. They do not change combat stats, ranges, cooldowns, movement, healing, shields, objective damage, RNG consumption, or deterministic event ordering. Final combined verification remains intentionally deferred at the user's request.

## Personality live-choice coverage invariants (2026-10-12)

- Personality may break an optional wave hold for an already-visible chase only after the existing safe-approach, no-abort, non-danger, visibility, tower, and commitment gates allow it. It cannot make an unsafe chase, dive, or chase through a protected turret perimeter valid.
- A personality-led reset requires the ordinary recall channel-safety predicate in addition to its preference. It cannot supersede an urgent defense, active objective/fight, recall cooldown, enemy proximity, threatening wave, dive evacuation, or tower exit requirement.
- A personality-led regroup is limited to a living IGL, a safe route, no local enemy, material separation, and no higher-priority defense/objective/fight/push. It is evaluated before optional camp farm only; it does not invent lanes, change farming limits, or alter movement speed.
- Early First Blood Hunter farm trade-off ends after 180 simulation seconds. Embermaw and Gravemarch optional starts both continue to require their pre-existing objective preference, health, wave, vision, group, and enemy-number gates.

## Ability documentation and movement-consistency invariants (2026-10-12)

- `abilityPresentation.ts` is a UI adapter over the existing champion kit, cast-type, range, ratio, and skill-progression rules. It may describe them, but it never chooses casts, changes combat physics, or duplicates execution. Standard Q/W/R panels in Champion Hub and Draft use this shared source; Kaelen's independently executable Conflux spells retain their dedicated spellbook.
- Solana Zenith Lance and Veyara Facet Dash are genuine simulation-time dashes, not instant position writes. Their control/damage and impact VFX occur at collision or endpoint; they remain subject to their existing legal cast, cooldown, mana, collision, interruption, terrain, tower-dive, retreat, and deterministic fixed-step rules. Kage/Inai relocation and Paxi's post-orb Jaunt remain genuine teleports and are not converted into dashes.
- Standard ultimate rank scaling uses the existing `abilityDamageMultiplier` table for both targeted and Raijin ground casts. Generic Skill 2 direct damage uses the same table. This aligns presentation with execution without changing authored base values or adding stat/range/cooldown exceptions.
- Full ability-executor audit, movement telemetry expansion, controlled visual checks, and the required final game/build checks are still pending at the user's direction to finish coding first.

## Ability documentation and movement-consistency follow-up (2026-10-12)

- Valerie's Vault Breaker Punch, Jaxon's Thundering Leap, and Brewmaw's Barrel Rush now advance with fixed simulation movement instead of direct coordinate snaps. Their damage and crowd control resolve only at a valid collision or endpoint.
- Dash traces are bounded and include cast origin, intended destination, actual endpoint, target, and collision/endpoint result. They are diagnostic only and do not add RNG, decision changes, or per-frame logging. Match Insights surfaces their completion events.
- Leap height and movement trails are rendering-only. They never alter the simulation path, hitbox, collision timing, replay determinism, map bounds, tower rules, recall, or objectives.
- Explicit teleports/blinks remain instant only when already authored. Mobility continues to require the ordinary AI eligibility, mana, cooldown, target, and protected safety gates. Verification remains deferred until coding completion as requested.
- Hard crowd control cancels an in-flight dash and emits one bounded `interrupted` movement trace. A movement step that would enter rock terrain stops at the existing terrain-safe resolver and emits one bounded `terrain` trace. Neither branch applies contact damage, consumes new RNG, or bypasses protected movement/tower rules.
- Dash state stores authored base damage only. `applyDamageToChampion` remains the sole owner of rank progression, ratios, items, amplification, mitigation, and true-damage resolution, preventing deferred impacts from receiving rank scaling twice.
- The live spectator cooldown group may present shared ability facts in a compact hover summary only. It does not create a second spell state or affect cooldowns, mana, cast selection, or simulation.
- Presentation records are cached by champion, slot, and rank. This keeps repeated live roster updates from rebuilding long mechanical descriptions; cached data remains read-only and gameplay-independent.
- Standard Skill 2 and ultimate call sites pass authored base damage to `applyDamageToChampion`; rank progression is applied exactly once in that shared resolver. This is a formula-consistency correction, not a new balance system: item ratios, amplification, mitigation, true damage, authored bases, and special multipliers remain under their existing owners. Solana Shieldbash's catalog base is 120, matching its pre-existing combat damage.
- Standard Q catalog records and descriptions identify their actual per-hit representation for Astra, Kyumi, Buck, Valkira, Kindra, and Cora. This documentation reconciliation does not change combat behavior, hit counts, projectile paths, or gameplay safety invariants.
- The ability inventory is source-derived, read-only diagnostic data. It lists 45 passives, 135 standard actives, and 10 Kaelen invoked spells with their existing executor, VFX/audio/AI routes. A status of `requires-live-verification` is observational only: it neither changes an ability nor treats static code review as visual proof. Champion Hub rank data is drawn exclusively from the shared presentation adapter.
- Movement presentation values are derived from the existing dash speed and duration. Solana Zenith Lance is a stun on valid dash contact; it is not documented as a root. These labels are explanatory only and leave movement, collision, combat timing, and safety gates unchanged.
- Draft's compact Q/W/R cards reuse the shared ability presentation summary, the same read-only source used by the dossier and spectator hover. This affects presentation only and cannot modify casting, resources, cooldowns, combat, AI, or protected gameplay behavior.

## Laravel cloud-persistence boundary (2026-10-13)

- Laravel stores authenticated account, club snapshot, and match-report data only. It is not an alternate arena simulator and cannot change champion mechanics, card stats, AI decisions, ability execution, RNG order, movement, objectives, dives, recalls, attribution, or any protected gameplay invariant.
- Browser storage remains an offline fallback. Cloud persistence is opt-in through an authenticated client and transfers roster/Starting 5/replay snapshots as data; it never grants gameplay authority to a network response.
- The Squad cloud panel is an explicit save/restore action, never an automatic decision or combat request. A restored roster still runs required-card reconciliation and requires exactly five valid starters before replacing the local lineup.

## Roster-wide ability ratio invariant (2026-10-13)

- Every damaging standard Q/W/R uses the shared role-authored 15â€“25% bonus-AD/AP ratio source: Mage 15/25, Marksman 25/15, Assassin 23/18, Fighter 22/16, Tank 18/15, Support 15/20. Explicit skill values are clamped inside that same envelope; zero-damage utility cannot become damaging through items.
- Ratios are applied once in the shared damage resolver, before the already-owned rank scaling, item amplification, mitigation, shields, and attribution. They do not change base damage, HP, range, cooldown, mana, movement, AI safety, tower/retreat rules, objective behavior, or RNG order.

## Arsenaldo Conflux invariants (2026-10-13)

- Arsenaldo is Kaelen's public Conflux identity only; stable Kaelen IDs, recipes, saved state, and internal references remain unchanged. Ten legacy three-orb recipes map one-to-one to the reworked spellbook.
- D/F is a two-entry FIFO invocation loadout, not a two-cast or two-spell-per-fight limit. Tactical plans may construct legal recipes, cast an available slot, reinvoke a later spell, and replan. Every orb still costs mana, every spell has its own cooldown/mana gate, and Conflux's level-based cooldown remains authoritative. A stale/invalid target, lost opportunity, tower abort, recall, death, unavailable mana, or cooldown cancels/replans rather than bypassing a gate.
- Hoarfrost's mark expires, has a three-hit follow-up threshold, consumes its stacks on a short root, and applies a target-specific lockout. Undertow/Pressure hit each target at most once per cast. Slows have explicit timer/magnitude caps; Ashen Hail stacks are capped and expire; zone damage has explicit duration/tick cadence and only applies while inside. No Arsenaldo effect grants indefinite CC or unbounded DoT.
- Equinox shields refresh rather than stack and do not grant invulnerability. Prism Warden is limited to one live summon per Arsenaldo, is killable, expires on its owner/lifetime, has bounded attacks and slow, and never targets structures. It does not alter waves, tower damage, objective secure, Nexus vulnerability, or ownership rules.
- Conflux planning may use IQ and existing personality traits only as decision preference. It cannot modify raw spell damage, CC duration, range, cooldown, mana regeneration, champion statistics, targetability, tower-risk assessment, retreat routing, objective contest priority, vision, or deterministic RNG order. Existing risk-based tower combat, objective champion-first contesting, steering/anti-spin, attribution, and parody alias invariants remain final authority.
- Each damaging invoked spell has an explicit authored ratio override in the bounded 15â€“22% bonus-AD and 15â€“25% AP range. It is passed once to the existing shared damage resolver; orb amp, rank scaling, mitigation, and normal item interactions retain their existing owners. Utility-only spells do not gain hidden damage scaling.

## Nexus finish commitment invariant (2026-10-13)

- An enemy Nexus may receive a dedicated finish commitment only while `canDamageNexus` confirms it is exposed and live attackers have a bounded, favorable authored-structure-DPS and survival window. This is a structure siege, not a champion tower dive. The calculation uses actual basic-attack structure damage/cadence, approach distance, Nexus volley pressure, and a bounded nearby-defender margin; it does not add damage, range, speed, invulnerability, ability-to-structure damage, or a second combat simulator.
- A viable finish outranks optional farm, wave clear unless it genuinely blocks the legal siege, side objectives, shopping/recall, regrouping, and unrelated champion chase. Critical allied-base defense and genuine lethal danger remain higher priority. The finish applies no global turret-safety exception: Nexus attack range, Nexus vulnerability, structure damage, defensive true damage, and normal terrain-aware retreat remain authoritative.
- Finish state is re-evaluated on material legal, attacker, damage-window, or survival changes and emits bounded `NEXUS_FINISH_*` diagnostics; it is never held by a blind long timer or a legacy dive-abort state.

## Risk-based tower combat and natural retreat invariant (2026-10-14)

- Per explicit user approval, the standalone Abort Dive subsystem is retired. There is no dive-abort state, fixed timeout, cooldown, forced evacuation vector, special speed, attack suppression, or abort-only UI/telemetry. Existing historical notes describing it are superseded by this section.
- Tower threat remains fully real: authored damage, true-damage behavior, range, cadence, target selection, minion aggro, terrain, structures, and death attribution are unchanged. Before a tower fight, the shared risk read estimates elimination and survival windows from real local state. IQ and personality influence acceptance only around marginal windows; they never alter gameplay stats or legal restrictions.
- A favorable tower risk can support an existing combat/all-in commitment. An unfavorable read avoids a new commitment, but does not force a unit to run or suppress a legal attack. Later disengagement uses the normal terrain-aware retreat router at normal movement speed. Decision commitments, target stickiness, action recovery, and facing deadband remain responsible for preventing oscillation.
- Nexus finishing remains an independently legal, bounded structure-siege decision. It may take legitimate match-ending risk but cannot bypass Nexus exposure, attack range, authored structure damage, defensive fire, or actual lethal-window evaluation.

## Utility decision migration boundary (2026-10-14)

- The user reconfirmed the latest risk-based tower combat rules; historical Abort Dive requirements in AGENTS.md are superseded. No removed abort state, speed bonus, forced evacuation or suppression may be restored by this migration.
- `utilityDecision.ts` rejects owner-invalid candidates before scoring. Normalized finite factors are bounded; danger/travel/resource/opportunity costs cannot become negative bonuses. Personality and mood are small decision contributions only. Valid higher-priority emergencies/finishes cannot be displaced by optional preferences. A commitment cannot retain an unavailable candidate or survive a changed context merely because its score is high.
- Stable caller order and bounded candidate counts preserve deterministic ties. No RNG, combat-stat modification, movement execution, ability executor or persistent controller is introduced. `decisionActionRecovery.ts` retains its twelve-target pass, retry blocks and existing target preference through the shared selector; full cross-kind macro arbitration is not implemented yet.
- Arsenaldo's corrected comparator ranks preferred plans ahead of unlisted plans, uses a symmetric tie-break, and prioritizes urgent protection over optional personality sequences. Spell availability, mana, cooldown, FIFO invocation and authored effects remain authoritative.
- Verification: 295 game tests plus 3 deterministic simulation tests, TypeScript and build passed; 256 target-selection equivalence cases passed. Live controlled 5v5, Conflux, attack/wave and spectator fixtures passed. Headless baseline and selector microbenchmark measurements are in `ai-overhaul-audit.md` and `ai-baseline.json`; neither establishes full live/headless parity or completion of the requested 40-scenario matrix. New tactical coordination, influence maps, broader memory/prediction, unified macro execution and learning remain future work under the current request.

## Residual macro selection boundary (2026-10-14)

- After the existing early protective and objective branches resolve, one residual action is selected among legal fight, wave, regroup, farm, and push candidates. The selected action carries a 0.65-second commitment only while its context remains valid. It cannot retain a candidate when a higher-priority legal emergency appears.
- Existing policy gates remain hard gates: this layer does not make a farm route safe, reveal enemies, authorize a tower crossing, start an objective, bypass a recall channel, or change any ability/structure mechanics. Nexus finish, urgent base defense, retreat, recall, relic handling, and objective-start execution are deliberately outside this residual pass until their owning executors are migrated together.
- Player personality remains a bounded preference contribution to legal regroup, wave, farm and push candidates. It does not change combat stats or override protected behavior.
- Verification for this migration: 300 game tests, 3 deterministic simulation tests, TypeScript, production build, and the controlled live 5v5 browser fixture passed. The fixture observed all ten units committed to combat, two deaths, and no legacy abort state.

## Recovery and objective-start arbitration boundary (2026-10-14)

- Recovery candidates enter the shared macro selector only after existing recall, defense, danger, tower, threat, relic, and Nexus checks decide they are legal. Danger and defend-related recalls are emergencies; ordinary safe recall and safe relic recovery are tactical. A 0.45-second context commitment prevents avoidable re-evaluation without retaining an unavailable action.
- Dragon and Gravemarch start/finish candidates enter the same selector only after their existing wave, vision, health, contest, combat, defense, and Nexus gates pass. Objective finishes use the finish tier. Normal starts use the tactical tier. The selector cannot create an objective route, vision, attack, ownership claim, or boss-damage exception.
- Objective contestation remains champion-first while the boss is above 32% health; only the existing low-health execution policy can select hitting the boss. Tower risk, natural retreat, true turret damage, attribution, deterministic tie ordering, and player-stat invariants remain unchanged.
- Verification: `npm.cmd run check:game` passed 301 game tests, 3 deterministic simulation tests, and TypeScript. `npm.cmd run build` passed with the existing chunk-size warning. The controlled browser fixture passed at five viewport sizes; its proactive 5v5 sample recorded all ten actors fighting, two deaths, and no legacy abort state.

## Shared team-intent coordination boundary (2026-10-14)

- A team may retain one prevalidated call for 0.65 seconds: defend, fight, objective finish, regroup, push, or hold. The deterministic coordinator rejects unavailable calls before scoring; a legal emergency defense and legal finish retain their existing priority.
- The call is a bounded preference only. It raises the coordination component of legal per-unit macro candidates. Supports interpret a fight call as regroup/peel, while other roles favor legal fight participation. It does not create targets, routes, vision, damage, casts, tower permission, objective permission, or movement exceptions.
- All local safety and mechanical owners remain authoritative, including champion-first objective contesting above 32%, tower-risk assessment, natural retreat, target recovery, action progress, and anti-spin commitments.
- Verification: `npm.cmd run check:game` passed 304 game tests, 3 deterministic simulation tests, and TypeScript. `npm.cmd run build` passed with the existing chunk-size warning. The controlled browser fixture passed at five viewport sizes; its 5v5 sample again recorded all ten actors fighting, two deaths, and no legacy abort state.

## Spatial influence boundary (2026-10-14)

- A read-only deterministic field measures enemy-champion and armed-structure threat, plus healthy allied and allied-structure cover, at a point or along a straight approach. It has no RNG, persistent memory, target revelation, pathfinding, or mechanics authority.
- Existing retreat safety gates remain mandatory. Only after they pass, the existing retreat ranking adds a bounded threat cost. Existing objective, fight, regroup, and push candidates similarly receive bounded scoring inputs only after their current owner-policy gates pass.
- The influence read cannot make an unsafe bush, route, tower dive, objective attempt, cast, or attack legal. Risk-based tower combat, natural retreat, champion-first objective contesting, attribution, anti-spin, and player-stat invariants remain authoritative.
- Per user direction, verification is deferred until all requested phases are implemented; no test, build, browser, or verification command ran after this phase.

## Tactical outlook boundary (2026-10-14)

- A deterministic short-horizon read estimates a legal candidate's travel time, local survival confidence, nearby support, and pressure from current visible state and spatial approach. It does not simulate damage, casts, cooldowns, movement results, fog, or RNG.
- Only existing legal fight, objective, and push candidates receive the bounded score contributions. The read cannot promote a rejected candidate, change priority tiers, manufacture a target, or override tower, retreat, objective, or executor rules.
- Per user direction, verification remains deferred until all requested phases are implemented; no test, build, browser, or verification command ran after this phase.

## Visible opponent tactical-memory boundary (2026-10-14)

- Each team may retain only its observed opponent movement-homeward (0.9 seconds) and observed recent damage focus (0.85 seconds). Memory is reset with the match and never records unseen units, hidden abilities, inferred locations, or unobserved cooldowns.
- Existing legal fight candidates may prefer a visibly retreating target; existing legal regroup candidates may prefer protection when opponents have visibly focused the actor. These are bounded score inputs after current hard gates, never target creation or authorization.
- Per user direction, verification remains deferred until all requested phases are implemented; no test, build, browser, or verification command ran after this phase.

## Post-fight conversion boundary (2026-10-14)

- After an existing won-fight gate, a bounded selector ranks only existing legal push, objective, reset, farm, and regroup conversions. It does not create any option or alter its underlying checks.
- Selected conversion contributes only to legal residual regroup, farm, and push scores. Objective, recall, route, combat, structure, and safety execution remain with their current owners.
- Per user direction, verification remains deferred until all requested phases are implemented.

## Bounded decision telemetry boundary (2026-10-14)

- Residual macro selection records a bounded 240-entry in-memory diagnostic history containing time, actor/team, selected context, and current candidate availability/reason. Diagnostics are not an input to any decision, mechanic, RNG source, or UI state.
- Per user direction, verification remains deferred until all requested phases are implemented.

- Controlled live fixtures passed attack windup/projectile/wave collision, Kindra range/smoke/respite values, fresh proactive 5v5 engagement, spectator behavior, and legacy-abort absence. Fixture mutations require a fresh combat fixture; a fresh rerun passed after Phase 6/7 inspection.
- This evidence does not prove generalized score quality, live/headless parity, or the complete requested scenario matrix.

## New decision-layer scenarios (2026-10-14)

- Focused scenarios passed for spatial threat/cover reads, supported versus exposed outlooks, visible-memory expiry/unseen-attacker rejection, and legal post-fight push selection without inventing a conversion.

## Live/headless parity boundary (2026-10-14)

- The source inventory in `liveHeadlessParity.ts` labels shared authored rules, live-only execution, and headless-simplified areas. A common rule import does not imply equal sequencing or outcome.
- Headless reports measure their own deterministic simplified executor. They cannot prove live macro, team-intent, spatial, outlook, memory, conversion, browser, terrain, or spectator behavior.

## ML preparation boundary (2026-10-14)

- Match exports contain a bounded residual-macro diagnostic history. Offline calibration summaries count offered and selected legal actions only. They cannot alter live weights, decisions, mechanics, RNG, or protected safety behavior.

## Local adaptive-policy boundary (2026-10-14)

- Completed live matches may update local bounded preferences for legal residual fight/wave/regroup/farm/push selections. The update is deterministic, outcome-based, and clamped. It is only a 0.04 utility component after hard gates and priority tiers; it cannot change mechanics or make an unavailable action legal.

## Player-card-aware learning boundary (2026-10-14)

- `adaptivePolicy.ts` version 2 uses card identity, IQ, teamfight/lane skill, flexibility, role fit, primary/secondary personalities, and current health/mana to score only existing legal residual fight/wave/regroup/farm/push candidates. Its output is clamped to [-1, 1] before the existing 0.04 learned utility weight. It cannot change priority tiers, combat stats, tower-risk policy, natural retreat, objective contest rules, targeting, routes, attacks, or casts.
- `aiDecisionTelemetry.ts` keeps at most 240 sampled decisions, suppressing unchanged per-tick repeats for two simulation seconds per actor. Training uses only selected legal actions that had an available same-priority alternative, with each actor's total match contribution normalized across its sampled records. Match winner remains an imperfect reward proxy; the model learns a bounded preference, not proven causal strategy or generalized headless parity. Version-1 local global weights migrate as action bias and corrupted storage is ignored or clamped.
- Affected code: `src/adaptivePolicy.ts`, `src/aiDecisionTelemetry.ts`, `src/components/AramMatchView.tsx`, `src/adaptivePolicy.test.mjs`. `npm.cmd run check:game` passed 313 game tests, 3 deterministic simulation tests, and TypeScript; `npm.cmd run build` passed with the existing large-chunk warning. No new controlled live browser match was run, so full-match learned-score effects remain unmeasured.

## Live learning evidence boundary (2026-10-14)

- A CDP runner (`scripts/compare-adaptive-live.mjs`) used the real arena at 4x with fixed lineups: one seed-47 training match, seed-51 empty versus trained policy, and a second seed-51 empty-policy run. See `docs/ml-live-evaluation.md` and its JSON evidence. The training match yielded nonzero per-card weights, but no action changed in 236 directly aligned held-out decision samples. Empty-policy seed-51 match duration varied from 6:34 to 8:39, so outcome differences are not attributable to the learned policy.
- This is no demonstrated benefit, not a safety-regression finding. The learned factor remains bounded and downstream of legal action gates. Future work requires same-state counterfactual selection and local action-outcome credit plus more held-out, side-swapped live matches before tuning or claiming effectiveness.

It records the counterfactual result but never executes it. `src/aiDecisionTelemetry.ts` attaches bounded 0â€“6-second actor health/gold/CS/kill/death/damage progress to the preceding sampled choice, and `src/decisionCalibration.ts` summarizes coverage and changed selections. This observational proxy is not a training reward or a safety permission.

## Executable macro choices and UI RNG isolation (2026-10-16)

Fight availability shares the existing executor range; objective-fight approaches retain their existing behavior. A defense fallback cannot be recorded as a residual push selection. Normal speeds, attack/cast ranges, priority/survival gates, tower policy, natural retreat and objective/Nexus rules remain authoritative.

Two complete fresh matches also matched exactly through 594.17 seconds, each with 98,782 residual passes without an unexpected intent mapping, including IGL regroup execution. No eligible safe close pair appeared in the two corrected full-match scouts; no learned-policy benefit or promotion is established. See `docs/ml-action-execution-audit.md`.

## Risk-based well and Nexus approach (2026-10-16)

- AI target acquisition and retained champion targets must respect the real healing well and lethal beam stripe (`baseSafetyRules.ts`, `combatDecision.ts`, `towerDiveRules.ts`, `AramMatchView.tsx`). Safe ranged poke at the healing fringe can remain available. Optional teamfight/support destinations must not pull another champion into the enemy beam. Existing well healing and 1,400 DPS beam are unchanged; these are AI decisions, not movement clamps or immunity.
- Fallback lane advance is bounded at the front attack position of the opposing Nexus, even for exposed Nexus/objective priority. A weak Nexus approach holds the normal perimeter regardless of a wave ahead. A live sealed Nexus remains a threat but cannot be attacked. `teamTempoRules.ts` and the live attackable/threat lists enforce these separate facts.
- `nexusFinishRules.ts` forecasts the actual five-pulse/reload phase, including held idle shots, before entering range. It counts individual authored basic attacks only after arrival/readiness and before predicted death, with incoming Nexus projectiles and the existing defender time margin. The live caller supplies fortification/wave, persistent AD, siege and marksman multipliers. Minions or a five-player group do not dilute Nexus damage. A healthy group, safe ranged finish or immediate survivable last hit can still finish; low Nexus HP alone cannot authorize an unsafe approach. This bounded estimate omits ability/minion damage, is conservative about shields/projectiles and approximates travel/delivery.
- Actual beam exposure or imminent lethal Nexus fire may interrupt a held fight via `decisionCommitment.ts`, followed by ordinary terrain-aware retreat. No dive-abort state, forced evacuation, elapsed dive deadline, attack suppression or rescue sprint is allowed. Champions deliberately placed behind a live guard can still die trying to retreat.
- Ten mirrored real-browser fixtures cover 15-second well/weak-Nexus decisions (no deaths; weak groups never enter Nexus range), sealed threat recognition, legal finishes and one-second normal escape from beam exposure. The sealed trap still kills all five despite their retreat decision. See `docs/base-safety-and-ml-gates.md` / `base-safety-live-evaluation.json` and `scripts/verify-base-safety-live.mjs` for exact setup and limits. `npm.cmd run check:game` passed 341 game tests, three simulations and TypeScript; production build passed with the existing chunk warning. Live fountain behavior is not claimed as full headless parity.

- Evaluation-only inspection in `safeMacroExperiment.ts` and `AramMatchView.tsx` reports first/all blocking gates and card/action utility gaps without changing assignment gates, legal actions or live weights. The paired inspector/runner exports coverage, aggregated by `scripts/analyze-macro-gates.mjs`.
- Two 180-second natural scouts (81 original, 82 swapped) yielded 74,308 residual decision ticks and zero fully eligible pairs. 68,415 lack a legal same-tier alternative. Of 304 otherwise-safe ticks, 302 farm/push ticks across five cards and two Flaker fight/regroup ticks exceed the existing 0.1 utility-gap limit. Counts are overlapping/repeated ticks, not independent outcomes. This identifies scarcity in these samples, not a causal ML effect. No alternate rollout, candidate promotion, learned-weight change or gate relaxation followed. Evidence/limits: `docs/base-safety-and-ml-gates.md`, `ml-gate-audit-81-original.json`, `ml-gate-audit-82-swapped.json`, `ml-gate-audit-analysis.json`.

## Completed-work evaluation and offline task learning (2026-10-16)

- Evaluation-only task hooks in `AramMatchView.tsx` record existing camp contributor shares, minion last-hit rewards and actual actor structure damage, capped to remaining HP and respecting sealed Nexus immunity. `taskOutcome.ts` excludes passive gold and movement; its authored thirty-second proxy gives up to 0.6 for actual camp/wave gold and 0.4 for structure damage, with any death yielding -1 even after respawn. It records time to first useful work but compares both arms at the same horizon. Natural retreat, target changes and commitment expiry remain authoritative; completing a task does not stop or hold an arm.
- `pairedMacroReplay.ts` and the paired runner allow up to thirty seconds, a second fixed draft/card set and opt-in paused batches up to 960 ticks. A pinned original assignment preserves short telemetry during long continuations; duplicate controls must match task events/outcomes and full captured state as well as existing checks. These controls remain unavailable in ordinary matches. The prior seed-81 predecision state is unchanged by the observer.
- `pairedTaskLearning.ts` is a separate offline reward-contrast regression. It uses the existing card/role/personality/resource features, requires independent training worlds and informative labels, excludes held-out/duplicate seeds and caps weight changes at 0.08. The collection plan fixed eight training and four held-out seed/draft/side cases; all twelve passed controls. Three training contrasts distinguished income/survival. Training loss fell from 0.241065 to 0.043782, and frozen-candidate held-out proxy prediction loss was 0.002689 (incumbent 0.109900; zero-contrast predictor 0.007479). Small-sample proxy prediction is not a match-win claim.
- Held-out evaluation over the captured legal candidates at the unchanged 0.04 learned factor changed zero actions, yielding zero extra reward/deaths. The candidate remains offline; no live promotion, gate relaxation, mechanical changes or weight increase occurred. A full-match policy trial and stronger independent evidence remain necessary before deployment. Exact plan, raw cases, frozen hashes and limits: `docs/ml-task-outcome-evaluation.md`, `docs/ml-task-outcome-plan.json`, `docs/ml-task-outcome-analysis.json`; collection/analysis scripts are `scripts/collect-task-outcomes.mjs` / `scripts/analyze-task-outcomes.mjs`.

Focused credit/death/horizon/fitting/leakage tests, twelve strict real-browser pairs, reward recomputation, frozen-data assertions, script syntax and `git diff --check` passed. Thirty seconds can miss later payoff/danger; the authored proxy omits some value and the browser/headless executors are not asserted fully equivalent. Protected risk-based combat, well/Nexus safety, ordinary retreat, attribution, objectives, anti-oscillation, stats and Cardrel remain intact.

## Evaluation-only learned action authority (2026-10-16)

- `rewardMacroDecision.ts` ranks only the trained farm/push pair after the existing safe experimental gates and a canonical highest-priority incumbent check. It switches only above a fixed 0.02 predicted task-reward advantage. This experimental ranking has different units from the ordinary 0.04 learned utility preference; normal matches keep their existing selector/factor. It cannot create a legal candidate, retain an expired commitment, override priority/survival/tower/defense/objective gates or affect combat stats/legality.
- `pairedMacroReplay.ts` requires an explicit reward arm/model. The live arena and runner execute the model's one chosen decision in a fourth thirty-second arm, retaining normal retreat and later replanning. The fourth arm must reach its executor and match the full same-action reference state, events and outcomes. Model bytes and training data stay frozen; new seeds are disjoint from prior task-training and held-out worlds.
- A known integration fixture verified an actual switch and is excluded from generalization evidence. Eight fresh cases (93â€“100; two drafts/sides) all passed four-arm validation: seven unchanged farm choices and one changed push. That push lost 75 camp gold (task reward -0.18), 0.2100 actor health fraction and 375 economic value relative to its control; no extra deaths occurred. There were zero fresh improvements, and the broad rubric also marked the changed case worse. **The candidate is not approved for live promotion.** Its feature vector lacks action-specific camp/route/cooldown/lane context, and this study does not establish a remedy or full-match benefit.
- See `docs/ml-reward-guidance-evaluation.md`, locked plan/model hashes, raw cases and `ml-reward-guidance-analysis.json`. Preparation/collection/validation scripts are `scripts/prepare-reward-guidance.mjs`, `collect-reward-guidance.mjs`, `analyze-reward-guidance.mjs`. The explicit unsupported-pair rejection was added after collection without changing any tested farm/push decision; analysis and required checks were repeated.
- Verification: `npm.cmd run check:game` passed 350 game tests, three simulations and TypeScript; build passed with the existing chunk warning. Focused selection/gate/model tests, known switching replay, eight fresh four-arm cases, frozen model/credit/state assertions, script syntax and `git diff --check` passed. Existing tower/well/Nexus protection, natural retreat, attribution, objectives, anti-oscillation, mechanics and Cardrel remain unchanged.

State transitions crossfade actions smoothly via Hermite ease weights (`t * t * (3 - 2 * t)`), synchronized local phases, and zero procedural bone overwrites.
- In `src/avatarCombatMotion.ts`, all 45 avatars are configured with distinct attack curves (`crush`, `thrust`, `cut`, `draw`, `shot`, `punch`, `weave`, `sweep`, `pulse`), casting curves (`invocation`, `storm`, `dance`, `earth_slam`, `brush`, `creature`, `martial`), hit reaction profiles (`heavy_brace`, `stagger_flinch`, `agile_recoil`, `creature_squash`), death fall profiles (`forward_kneel`, `backward_stagger`, `side_graceful`, `creature_crash`), and turning dynamics.
- Core archetypes established and verified:
  - Melee: Solana (paladin shield parry + spear thrust, forward kneel death), Kaolin (overhead heavy hammer heave + seismic slam, heavy brace), Vi (piston gauntlet straight punch, boxer recovery).
  - Ranged: Astra (recurve bow draw-to-cheek, bow cant release, quiver recovery), Buck (dual gunslinger draw, alternating muzzle-flip recoil), Senna (heavy cannon hoist, massive shoulder recoil).
  - Caster: Kyumi (circular spirit orb weaves, twin palm pulse), Invoker/Kaelen (celestial invocation, arched spine, cataclysm thrust), Hweilin (calligraphic 3D brush flourish).
  - Creature: Puck (aerial loop, wing pulse burst, aerial spiral fall), Largo (deep squat, spring tongue snap, flat belly flop), Skybreaker (chassis flak bursts, 22Â° aerodynamic bank into turn).
- Presentation only: no changes to simulation step ordering, RNG consumption, collision, damage, health, movement speeds, tower dive safety thresholds, or gameplay invariants.
- Verification: `npm run check:game` passed all 401 game tests, 3 multi-seed deterministic simulations, and TypeScript checking. `npm run build` succeeded.

## 2026-10-21: Automated 3D Character Rigging Pipeline Inspired by Meshy AI

Implemented an automated 3D character rigging, alignment, joint landmark detection, skinning, weapon attachment, and animation retargeting pipeline inspired by Meshy AI.

- **Pipeline Stages:** `CHARACTER_GENERATED â†’ ASSET_VALIDATION â†’ AUTO_ALIGNMENT â†’ LANDMARK_DETECTION â†’ SKELETON_GENERATION â†’ SKINNING â†’ WEAPON_ATTACHMENT â†’ RIG_VALIDATION â†’ ANIMATION_SETUP â†’ READY`.
- **Key Features:**
  - Strict generation contract enforcing neutral T-pose and zero merged weapons in body mesh.
  - Automatic model centering, grounding ($y=0$), height normalization (70 units), and sagittal depth asymmetry forward-facing orientation estimation.
  - Detection of 22 anatomical landmarks with bilateral sagittal symmetry ($X = -X$) and confidence scoring.
  - 18-bone standard humanoid skeleton construction with native game helper nodes (`avatar-root`, `left-arm`, `right-arm`, `leg:-1`, `leg:1`, `outfit`, `right-grip`) for zero-exception game runtime compatibility.
  - Smooth vertex skinning via bone capsule projection and Gaussian heat falloff with strictly normalized 4-weight glTF limit.
  - Modular weapon sockets (`right_hand_socket`, `left_hand_socket`, `weapon_tip`, `weapon_muzzle`) and procedural 3D weapon meshes for all 6 MOBA weapon classes.
  - 7 standard retargeted animation clips (`Idle`, `Walk`, `Run`, `Attack`, `Cast`, `Hit`, `Death`).
  - Automated 10-point rig validation suite.
  - Interactive Meshy-style 3D studio (`MeshyRiggingStudio.tsx`) accessible from `ChampionHubView.tsx`.
- **Invariants Protected:**
  - Tower dive limits and evacuation subsystems unchanged.
  - 10-second turret attribution window and execution splits unchanged.
  - Opponent objective contestation priorities unchanged.
  - Parody aliases (Cardrel) and champion stats/hitboxes unchanged.
- **Verification:**
  - `npm run check:game`: 485/485 unit tests passed, 3/3 batch simulations passed, `tsc -b` passed.
  - `npm run build`: Production web build passed cleanly.
  - `meshyRiggingPipeline.test.mjs`: 8/8 comprehensive pipeline subtests passed.

### 2026-10-27: Phase 3 — Spatial Audio & Sound Effects for 3D Combat

- Implemented **Spatial Audio & 3D Combat Sound System** in `audio.ts`:
  - Stereo panning and distance attenuation: `calculateSpatialPan(worldX, cameraX, viewportHalfW)` and `calculateSpatialGain(worldX, worldY, cameraX, cameraY, maxDist)`.

    - `playDragonRoar(worldX, cameraX)`: Infernal dragon roar with sweeping pitch modulation and resonant filter envelope.

### 2026-10-27: Phase 4 — LoL-Style Spectator Camera & Controls Polish

- Implemented **LoL-Style Spectator Camera System & Controls Polish** in `AramMatchView.tsx`:
  - **Champion Lock-On Keys**:
    - Keys `1`–`5`: Instantly locks camera onto Blue Team Champions 1 to 5.
    - Keys `6`–`9` & `0`: Instantly locks camera onto Red Team Champions 1 to 5 (Key `0` targets Red 5th champion or resets to arena view).
  - **Dynamic Follow Camera**:
    - Smoothly follows locked champion's position (`x = champ.x, y = champ.y + 60`) as they fight, dodge, kite, and maneuver in 3D.
    - Automatically unlocks and returns to auto teamfight action camera upon champion death.
  - **Spacebar Recenter**:
    - Pressing `Spacebar` releases any locked champion and recenters on the active teamfight action center.
  - **Drag Inertia & Damping**:
    - Computed continuous drag velocity `panVelocityRef` on user pan.
    - Applied exponential decay damping (`0.88` per frame) on mouse release for fluid, silky-smooth RTS camera gliding.
  - **Roster Portrait Integration**:
    - Updated `focusUnit(unit)` to lock camera tracking onto clicked roster portraits with audio feedback.
